<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-variational-letters** [claim](#stmt-entropy-foundations-variational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-foundations-variational): Convex minimization and Gibbs duality, symmetrised over permutations. -> exchangeable letters with uniform marginals and bounded noisy cost
- **step-cost-rules** [claim](#stmt-entropy-foundations-cost-rules) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-foundations-cost-rules): Chain rules for entropy under projection and conditioning. -> rules for keeping the same error
- **step-conditional-marginal** [claim](#stmt-entropy-foundations-exchangeable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-foundations-exchangeable): Monotone conditional entropies, random prefix length and Pinsker inequality. -> conditional marginal with nearly independent short blocks
- **step-context-bounds** [claim](#stmt-entropy-foundations-context-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-foundations-context-bounds): Entropy continuity under total variation and monotonicity under refinement. -> uniform bounds on exact-sum quantities
- **step-block-lifting** [claim](#stmt-entropy-foundations-block-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-foundations-block-cost): Lifting row sums to full vectors by conditional sampling. -> refinement monotonicity of the noisy block cost
- **step-thinning** [claim](#stmt-entropy-foundations-thinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-foundations-thinning): Tagging, weighted acceptance and mixing with a filling law. -> low-end noisy cost bound
- **step-gluing** [claim](#stmt-entropy-refinement-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-refinement-gluing): Independent minimizing trials glued and compared through entropy identities. -> cost difference paid by block observation
- **step-search** [claim](#stmt-entropy-refinement-search) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-refinement-search): Nested macroblock and subblock search with skip rules and two telescopes. -> context and index with controlled noisy cost
- **step-compression** [claim](#stmt-lemma-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-entropy-refinement-search-2): Freeze a context stack, find a noisy-sum atom, restrict to a phase region and prune to a subset. -> retained subset, center and phase volume bound
- **step-thermal-monotonicity** [claim](#stmt-lemma-thermal-monotonicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-thermal-monotonicity): Griffiths-Ginibre covariance expansion on the circle. -> form-order monotonicity and envelope
- **step-spectral-update** [claim](#stmt-lemma-spectral-update) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-spectral-update): Iterated application of the compression lemma and selection of a flat coefficient range. -> regular or cheap update with gap control
- **step-evidence** [claim](#stmt-prop-thermal-evidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-thermal-evidence): Posterior denominator bound and clone moment estimates. -> reference and true-law evidence bounds
- **step-live-budgets** [claim](#stmt-prop-live-budgets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-live-budgets): Gap telescoping, supermartingales and change of measure. -> live budgets
- **step-capture** [claim](#stmt-prop-capture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-07-activation): Trimmed masks, polynomial products, edge charging and snapshot comparison. -> frequency lists, snapshots and activation
- **step-path-estimates** [claim](#stmt-prop-vector-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-vector-path): Predictable sign inequality, scalar potential and vector norm telescoping with checkpoints. -> path estimates simultaneous over representatives
- **step-dyadic-estimate** [claim](#stmt-prop-dyadic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-paths-simultaneous-2): Plateau decomposition, decoding to scalar modulation and weight summation. -> dyadic bound linear in the height parameter
- **step-good-set** [claim](#stmt-thm-good-set) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-good-set): Translation and scale averaging of Haar shifts to the Hilbert kernel. -> good-set integral bound
- **step-bounded-inputs** [claim](#stmt-prop-fourier-bounded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fourier-bounded): Fejér approximation and convergence in measure. -> convergence for bounded functions
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fourier-bounded-2): Height-layer decomposition, Borel-Cantelli and Tonelli. -> almost-everywhere convergence in the Orlicz class
- **step-weak-l1** [claim](#stmt-cor-fourier-weak-l1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fourier-weak-l1): Stein's theorem on translation-commuting operators. -> weak-type maximal bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-kernel-constant**: The correlation integral of the two Haar profiles, whose value fixes the nonzero Hilbert-kernel multiple in the translation and scale averaging, equals the exact rational number minus one eighth; it is a finite piecewise-linear integral over four quarter intervals.
- **inv-kernel-odd**: The profile correlation kernel is an odd function, so its averaged kernel has a cotangent-type singular part with the constant above as coefficient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-l1-convergence**: Treat the finite kernel computation or the main convergence theorem as evidence that Fourier partial sums converge almost everywhere for arbitrary integrable functions. -> Almost-everywhere convergence fails in the integrable class, so any pipeline built on this assumption can diverge.
- **anti-global-integral**: Use the truncated maximal integral bound as a bound over the whole circle. -> The estimate is only over the good set where the spatial maximal density is controlled; the removed exceptional set is not covered.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The main theorems are analytic and non-constructive, but the kernel-constant calculation is a finite exact computation: integrate a piecewise-constant profile against the piecewise-linear primitive of the Haar step over four quarter intervals. Panel 3 implements and certifies only this finite step (it does not certify the convergence theorem).

```python
# INPUTS: four integers a b c d on stdin, the values of a piecewise-constant profile on the four quarters of the unit interval
# PRECONDITIONS: all four tokens are integers; the step function equals +1 on the first half and -1 on the second half
# OUTPUTS: one line with the exact rational value of the integral of the profile times the primitive of the step function
# TIME: constant number of operations
# SPACE: constant
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Python fractions
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def main():
    a, b, c, d = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
    H = [Fraction(0), Fraction(1, 4), Fraction(1, 2), Fraction(1, 4), Fraction(0)]
    total = Fraction(0)
    for i, gv in enumerate((a, b, c, d)):
        total += gv * (H[i] + H[i+1])/2 * Fraction(1, 4)
    print(total)

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a qualitative almost-everywhere convergence theorem. It gives no executable specification, and the constants are not made explicit. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statements hold up to null sets and are asymptotic in the partial-sum index, so they say nothing about frame-level fidelity in a real-time simulation. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constants are only generic ('can change from line to line'), so no tool can compute a numeric tolerance from them. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite dyadic procedure is a proof device that only yields an integral bound. It selects no output and gives no cost model, so there is nothing to implement. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Only bookkeeping lists inside the proof exist; no data structure with an operation interface or complexity guarantee is proposed. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no arithmetic pipeline, precision analysis or throughput model that a hardware design could use. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Information-theoretic tools appear only as proof machinery for convergence, and no adversary model or security reduction is stated. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss, generalisation statement or sample complexity is treated; the probabilistic arguments serve only the analytic estimate. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Parameters are chosen in a fixed order only to make asymptotic inequalities close; no objective is optimised and no feasibility or approximation guarantee is given. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The two sampling laws are probabilistic devices inside one proof; there are no nodes, messages, failures or consistency requirements. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only conceivable use is as a stated target in a formal-proof fixture, which is not a construction contained in the paper. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-orlicz-class-only** (hypothesis): The convergence conclusion is proved only for complex-valued functions in the logarithmically weighted Orlicz class on the circle. The paper itself recalls that it fails throughout the integrable class. Blocked use: ['Asserting almost-everywhere convergence of symmetric partial sums for an arbitrary integrable function', 'Applying the theorem to a signal whose only known regularity is integrability']
- **limit-full-sequence-qualitative** (formal_scope): The convergence is qualitative: it holds off a null set that depends on the function and is stated for the full sequence of partial-sum indices as the index tends to infinity. No rate, finite-index error bound or exceptional-set description is given. Blocked use: ['Bounding the error of a truncation at a fixed finite order', 'Deducing uniform convergence or control of overshoot near discontinuities']
- **limit-good-set-only** (edge_case): The truncated maximal integral bound controls the maximal partial sum only on the good set where the spatial maximal density is under control. The removed exceptional set is handled separately, by a measure bound that is small only as the height parameter grows. Blocked use: ['Using the linear integral bound as a bound over the whole circle', 'Applying it to inputs that are not nonnegative, of mass one and bounded by the exponential of the height parameter']
- **limit-large-height-threshold** (hypothesis): The good-set estimate holds only for height parameters above an absolute threshold, and the preliminary conventions require the parameter to be sufficiently large with a threshold that may depend on earlier fixed constants. Blocked use: ['Invoking the estimate at moderate or small height parameters', 'Claiming the threshold is explicit or computable from the paper']
- **limit-constants-not-explicit** (approximation): Numerical constants are generic and may change from line to line, so every bound is an order-of-magnitude statement with no explicit prefactor. Blocked use: ['Computing a numeric tolerance, threshold or stopping rule from the stated bounds', 'Comparing the constants with those of other results']
- **limit-finite-depth-dyadic** (edge_case): The delayed Haar estimate is proved for a finite depth and a finite frequency cutoff, with constants independent of both. The statement for the supremum over all integer modulations is obtained only as a consequence, by passing to the limit. Blocked use: ['Treating the finite dyadic procedure as an implementable frequency-selection routine', 'Reading the bound as a statement about an infinite-depth tree constructed explicitly']
- **limit-weak-type-nonuniform-operator-bounds** (edge_case): In the proof of the weak-type corollary, the individual partial-sum operators are bounded on the Orlicz space only with bounds that depend on the index. The weak-type bound comes from Stein's theorem together with the convergence theorem, not from per-index control. Blocked use: ['Using per-index operator norms of the partial sums as uniform in the index', 'Replacing the weak-type bound by a strong-type bound on the Orlicz space']
- **limit-no-effective-content** (unknown): The paper states no algorithm, complexity model or effective version of the convergence theorem, and gives no explicit measure of the exceptional null set. Blocked use: ['Claiming an effective or computable modulus of convergence', 'Claiming a machine-checked formalisation from the paper alone']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 075 (`lean/docs/075.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Almost-everywhere Fourier convergence in L log L](../../preprints/Almost-everywhere-Fourier-convergence-in-L-log-L-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The $L\log L$ Fourier-convergence conjecture asks whether ordinary symmetric Fourier partial sums converge almost everywhere at this integrability scale. The formalization proves this for every measurable complex-valued function on $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/(2\pi\mathbb Z)$ satisfying $\int_{\mathbb T}|f|\log(2+|f|)<\infty$. Outside one measurable null set,
> $\sum_{k=-N}^{N}\widehat f(k)e^{ikx}\to f(x)$ along the full sequence $N\to\infty$.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `circlePeriod_pos` | instance |
| `paperMain` | theorem |

Comparator source `FourierLLogL.lean` (821 bytes, sha256 `c4d23b5ee24bd446a0524df1e3af08c7ea7d293e079cc8c8d6030113bdb5ab3f`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

open MeasureTheory Filter
open scoped BigOperators Topology

namespace OAI.FourierLLogL

instance circlePeriod_pos : Fact (0 < 2 * Real.pi) :=
  ⟨mul_pos (by norm_num) Real.pi_pos⟩

/-- Almost-everywhere convergence of all symmetric Fourier partial sums in L log L. -/
theorem paperMain (f : AddCircle (2 * Real.pi) → ℂ)
    (hfm : AEStronglyMeasurable f AddCircle.haarAddCircle)
    (hfi : Integrable (fun x ↦ ‖f x‖ * Real.log (2 + ‖f x‖))
      AddCircle.haarAddCircle) :
    ∃ E : Set (AddCircle (2 * Real.pi)),
      MeasurableSet E ∧ AddCircle.haarAddCircle E = 0 ∧ ∀ x ∉ E,
        Tendsto
          (fun N : ℕ ↦
            ∑ k ∈ Finset.Icc (-(N : ℤ)) (N : ℤ), fourierCoeff f k * fourier k x)
          atTop (𝓝 (f x)) := by
  sorry

end OAI.FourierLLogL
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the ordinary symmetric Fourier partial sums of every complex-valued function in $`L\log L`$ on the circle converge almost everywhere to the function along the full sequence. This establishes the classical $`L\log L`$ sufficiency conjecture.
